My appetite just hasn’t been normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any unintentional weight loss recently, or have you lost weight without trying to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been weighing myself or anything, but my jeans are definitely looser. I noticed it because I was digging around for this belt I used to wear—couldn’t find it, of course, because our closet is kind of a disaster—and I realized my pants weren’t sitting right.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been trying to lose weight. I just keep missing meals, like breakfast turns into lunch and then I don’t feel like eating lunch either. Then at dinner I’ll just pick at it, even when my wife made something good. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost some weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite or a lack of interest in eating, even when you know you should eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much it. And now my jeans are getting loose, which is kind of what made me think, okay, maybe this is actually something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, most days lately I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, flat feeling already there. I’ll hold it and kind of strum a little, and then put it back down. So… yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down, and things aren’t really lifting me like they usually would.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say I get no pleasure from them. That sounds kind of absolute.

Like with guitar, sometimes I still like the feel of it, or I’ll play one little thing and think, okay, that sounded decent. But then I get stuck messing with the tuning or thinking the strings sound dull, and then I’m looking up whether I should change them, and suddenly I’m not really playing anymore.

With my wife… I mean, I like being around her. Sometimes we’ll be sitting together and it’s nice, but I’m also not always fully… there, I guess. Like I’ll start thinking about whether we need groceries or if the dishwasher is making that weird noise again.

So, no, I don’t think it’s like zero pleasure. It’s just hard for me to tell how much I’m actually enjoying things versus just kind of being present for them.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I mean, I didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but the past few weeks my eating’s been weird.

Like I’ll realize it’s late afternoon and I haven’t had breakfast or lunch, not because I’m trying to skip them, I just… didn’t really get around to it. And then at dinner, even if my wife made something, I’ll kind of pick at it. Which makes me feel bad, honestly, because she’ll make food and I’m sitting there moving it around like a kid.

And it’s probably not just in my head, because my jeans have been feeling looser lately. Although one pair always stretched out weird after I washed them wrong, so I don’t know if that counts, but… yeah, the eating being off has been pretty regular for a few weeks.

I just keep missing meals, like breakfast turns into lunch and then I don’t feel like eating lunch either. Then at dinner I’ll just pick at it, even when my wife made something good. Like before anything even happens. The apartment feels really quiet in a way that gets to me, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd usually I’d pick up my guitar for a bit, even just messing around with the same few chords—my wife jokes I only know like twelve songs, which is not totally fair—but lately even that doesn’t really do much. I’ll hold it and kind of strum a little, and then put it back down. So… yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down, and things aren’t really lifting me like they usually would.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. But then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I forgot to take the trash out, and then I’m thinking about whether the trash bags are the wrong size because they keep slipping down into the bin.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. It’s kind of hard to tell what counts, because sometimes I’ll think about doing something and then not actually do it, which is different than deciding I don’t like it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction from things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar or spending time with your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I get no pleasure from them. I mean, I didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but the past few weeks my eating’s been weird.\n\nLike I’ll realize it’s late afternoon and I haven’t had breakfast or lunch, not because I’m trying to skip them, I just… didn’t really get around to it. My appetite just hasn’t been normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any unintentional weight loss recently, or have you lost weight without trying to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I haven’t been weighing myself or anything, but my jeans are definitely looser. I noticed it because I was digging around for this belt I used to wear—couldn’t find it, of course, because our closet is kind of a disaster—and I realized my pants weren’t sitting right.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been trying to lose weight. I just keep missing meals, like breakfast turns into lunch and then I don’t feel like eating lunch either. Then at dinner I’ll just pick at it, even when my wife made something good. So… yeah, I guess I’ve lost some weight without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite or a lack of interest in eating, even when you know you should eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much it. For the past few weeks I just haven’t really been interested in eating. Like I’ll mean to have breakfast, then I get distracted or whatever and suddenly it’s lunchtime, and then lunch doesn’t sound good either.\n\nDinner’s the same—I’ll sit down, and my wife cooked something, so I feel like I should eat it, but I mostly just pick at it. And now my jeans are getting loose, which is kind of what made me think, okay, maybe this is actually something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, most days lately I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, flat feeling already there. Like before anything even happens. The apartment feels really quiet in a way that gets to me, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd usually I’d pick up my guitar for a bit, even just messing around with the same few chords—my wife jokes I only know like twelve songs, which is not totally fair—but lately even that doesn’t really do much. I’ll hold it and kind of strum a little, and then put it back down. So… yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down, and things aren’t really lifting me like they usually would.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days lately, I wake up and it’s already there, this heavy, flat kind of feeling. Like nothing’s happened yet, but the day already feels… dim, I guess.\n\nAnd the apartment being quiet makes it worse somehow. Which is funny because I used to like quiet—like, I’d sit with my guitar and mess around, and that was enough. Lately I’ll pick it up and it just doesn’t really do anything for me, so I put it back down. So yeah, I’d say it’s been most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more tired than usual, or like you don’t have the energy to do things, even simple tasks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t feel like I’m bouncing with energy or anything, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s a big tired thing.\n\nSome days I just kind of sit there longer than I mean to—like I’ll look at the dishes and think, okay, I should do that, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether we need new sponges, because the blue ones fall apart too fast, and then I’m on my phone looking up reviews for sponges, which is ridiculous. But I don’t know if that’s tired exactly or just… not getting moving.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, have you felt restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, I think. Not so much like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes me a weird amount of time to start.\n\nLike I’ll have my laptop open in front of me, and I’m just sitting there staring at it, and it’s not even that I’m doing anything else. I’ll think, okay, type something, or plug in the keyboard—because I have this separate keyboard I like, the keys feel better than the laptop ones—and then I just… don’t move my hands for ages. It’s kind of embarrassing how long I can sit there before doing something really simple.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: I… don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I get really low, but “hurting myself” feels like a pretty specific thing, and I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making plans or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I’ll have these sort of blank thoughts, like, what am I even doing today, or what’s the point of getting up just to move from the bed to the couch. But then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I forgot to take the trash out, and then I’m thinking about whether the trash bags are the wrong size because they keep slipping down into the bin.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. Not in a clear “I’m going to hurt myself” way.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely stopped doing things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar, even for just a little while?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say completely stopped. Like… with guitar, I’ll still pick it up sometimes, but then I’ll spend ten minutes tuning it, or looking for the pick I like—the gray one, not the flimsy ones—and then I don’t really end up playing much.\n\nSo maybe I’m doing less, but “completely stopped” feels too strong. It’s kind of hard to tell what counts, because sometimes I’ll think about doing something and then not actually do it, which is different than deciding I don’t like it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction from things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar or spending time with your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I get no pleasure from them. That sounds kind of absolute.\n\nLike with guitar, sometimes I still like the feel of it, or I’ll play one little thing and think, okay, that sounded decent. But then I get stuck messing with the tuning or thinking the strings sound dull, and then I’m looking up whether I should change them, and suddenly I’m not really playing anymore.\n\nWith my wife… I mean, I like being around her. Sometimes we’ll be sitting together and it’s nice, but I’m also not always fully… there, I guess. Like I’ll start thinking about whether we need groceries or if the dishwasher is making that weird noise again.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s like zero pleasure. It’s just hard for me to tell how much I’m actually enjoying things versus just kind of being present for them.\n\nDoctor: Has your lack of appetite or interest in eating been happening almost every day for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but the past few weeks my eating’s been weird.\n\nLike I’ll realize it’s late afternoon and I haven’t had breakfast or lunch, not because I’m trying to skip them, I just… didn’t really get around to it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I think so. It’s not like I’m confused about where I am or anything, but when I try to do something that takes actual focus, I get stuck.

Like if I’m working on some code, I’ll read the same line over and over and still not really know what I’m looking at, even though I know I should. Or with music, if I’m trying to arrange something, I’ll replay the same eight bars a bunch of times and then I still can’t decide what should come next.

And then I start thinking maybe the problem is the sound, or the plug-in, or whether I organized the folder wrong—because I have these folders named terribly, honestly—and then I’m not making the decision anymore. I’m just circling around it. So yeah, thinking clearly has felt harder lately.

I mean, I get really low, but “hurting myself” feels like a pretty specific thing, and I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making plans or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I’ll have these sort of blank thoughts, like, what am I even doing today, or what’s the point of getting up just to move from the bed to the couch. But then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I forgot to take the trash out, and then I’m thinking about whether the trash bags are the wrong size because they keep slipping down into the bin.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. Not in a clear “I’m going to hurt myself” way.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely stopped doing things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar, even for just a little while?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say completely stopped. Like… with guitar, I’ll still pick it up sometimes, but then I’ll spend ten minutes tuning it, or looking for the pick I like—the gray one, not the flimsy ones—and then I don’t really end up playing much.\n\nSo maybe I’m doing less, but “completely stopped” feels too strong. It’s kind of hard to tell what counts, because sometimes I’ll think about doing something and then not actually do it, which is different than deciding I don’t like it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction from things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar or spending time with your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I get no pleasure from them. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… most days, I think. It’s kind of there when I wake up, like this heavy, flat feeling before anything’s even happened.

The apartment feels really quiet, even though it’s always kind of quiet in the morning—except the fridge makes this clicking noise sometimes, which I keep meaning to figure out—but lately it just feels… emptier, I guess. And even if I pick up my guitar, which usually changes the mood at least a little, it doesn’t really lift it.

So yeah, not every single second, but most of the day, most days lately.

Dinner too—I’ll just sort of pick at it, even when my wife made something good, which makes me feel bad because she actually puts effort into it. \n\nAnd my jeans are starting to feel loose, which I noticed because I was looking for this old belt the other day—anyway, yeah, that’s the main thing. I just keep missing meals, like breakfast turns into lunch and then I don’t feel like eating lunch either. Then at dinner I’ll just pick at it, even when my wife made something good. And now my jeans are getting loose, which is kind of what made me think, okay, maybe this is actually something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, most days lately I wake up and it’s like there’s this heavy, flat feeling already there. Like before anything even happens. The apartment feels really quiet in a way that gets to me, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd usually I’d pick up my guitar for a bit, even just messing around with the same few chords—my wife jokes I only know like twelve songs, which is not totally fair—but lately even that doesn’t really do much. I’ll hold it and kind of strum a little, and then put it back down. So… yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty down, and things aren’t really lifting me like they usually would.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days lately, I wake up and it’s already there, this heavy, flat kind of feeling. Like nothing’s happened yet, but the day already feels… dim, I guess.\n\nAnd the apartment being quiet makes it worse somehow. Which is funny because I used to like quiet—like, I’d sit with my guitar and mess around, and that was enough. Lately I’ll pick it up and it just doesn’t really do anything for me, so I put it back down. So yeah, I’d say it’s been most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more tired than usual, or like you don’t have the energy to do things, even simple tasks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, I don’t feel like I’m bouncing with energy or anything, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s a big tired thing.\n\nSome days I just kind of sit there longer than I mean to—like I’ll look at the dishes and think, okay, I should do that, and then somehow I’m thinking about whether we need new sponges, because the blue ones fall apart too fast, and then I’m on my phone looking up reviews for sponges, which is ridiculous. But I don’t know if that’s tired exactly or just… not getting moving.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, have you felt restless or fidgety, like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… slower, I think. Not so much like I can’t sit still. It’s more like everything takes me a weird amount of time to start.\n\nLike I’ll have my laptop open in front of me, and I’m just sitting there staring at it, and it’s not even that I’m doing anything else. I’ll think, okay, type something, or plug in the keyboard—because I have this separate keyboard I like, the keys feel better than the laptop ones—and then I just… don’t move my hands for ages. It’s kind of embarrassing how long I can sit there before doing something really simple.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: I… don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I get really low, but “hurting myself” feels like a pretty specific thing, and I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making plans or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I’ll have these sort of blank thoughts, like, what am I even doing today, or what’s the point of getting up just to move from the bed to the couch. But then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I forgot to take the trash out, and then I’m thinking about whether the trash bags are the wrong size because they keep slipping down into the bin.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. Not in a clear “I’m going to hurt myself” way.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely stopped doing things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar, even for just a little while?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say completely stopped. Like… with guitar, I’ll still pick it up sometimes, but then I’ll spend ten minutes tuning it, or looking for the pick I like—the gray one, not the flimsy ones—and then I don’t really end up playing much.\n\nSo maybe I’m doing less, but “completely stopped” feels too strong. It’s kind of hard to tell what counts, because sometimes I’ll think about doing something and then not actually do it, which is different than deciding I don’t like it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction from things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar or spending time with your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I get no pleasure from them. That sounds kind of absolute.\n\nLike with guitar, sometimes I still like the feel of it, or I’ll play one little thing and think, okay, that sounded decent. But then I get stuck messing with the tuning or thinking the strings sound dull, and then I’m looking up whether I should change them, and suddenly I’m not really playing anymore.\n\nWith my wife… I mean, I like being around her. Sometimes we’ll be sitting together and it’s nice, but I’m also not always fully… there, I guess. Like I’ll start thinking about whether we need groceries or if the dishwasher is making that weird noise again.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s like zero pleasure. It’s just hard for me to tell how much I’m actually enjoying things versus just kind of being present for them.\n\nDoctor: Has your lack of appetite or interest in eating been happening almost every day for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s more like everything takes me a weird amount of time to start.\n\nLike I’ll have my laptop open in front of me, and I’m just sitting there staring at it, and it’s not even that I’m doing anything else. I’ll think, okay, type something, or plug in the keyboard—because I have this separate keyboard I like, the keys feel better than the laptop ones—and then I just… don’t move my hands for ages. It’s kind of embarrassing how long I can sit there before doing something really simple.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: I… don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I get really low, but “hurting myself” feels like a pretty specific thing, and I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making plans or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I’ll have these sort of blank thoughts, like, what am I even doing today, or what’s the point of getting up just to move from the bed to the couch. But then I’ll get distracted by something stupid, like realizing I forgot to take the trash out, and then I’m thinking about whether the trash bags are the wrong size because they keep slipping down into the bin.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. Not in a clear “I’m going to hurt myself” way.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely stopped doing things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar, even for just a little while?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say completely stopped. Like… with guitar, I’ll still pick it up sometimes, but then I’ll spend ten minutes tuning it, or looking for the pick I like—the gray one, not the flimsy ones—and then I don’t really end up playing much.\n\nSo maybe I’m doing less, but “completely stopped” feels too strong. It’s kind of hard to tell what counts, because sometimes I’ll think about doing something and then not actually do it, which is different than deciding I don’t like it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t get any pleasure or satisfaction from things you used to enjoy, like playing guitar or spending time with your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I get no pleasure from them. That sounds kind of absolute.\n\nLike with guitar, sometimes I still like the feel of it, or I’ll play one little thing and think, okay, that sounded decent. But then I get stuck messing with the tuning or thinking the strings sound dull, and then I’m looking up whether I should change them, and suddenly I’m not really playing anymore.\n\nWith my wife… I mean, I like being around her. Sometimes we’ll be sitting together and it’s nice, but I’m also not always fully… there, I guess. Like I’ll start thinking about whether we need groceries or if the dishwasher is making that weird noise again.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s like zero pleasure. It’s just hard for me to tell how much I’m actually enjoying things versus just kind of being present for them.\n\nDoctor: Has your lack of appetite or interest in eating been happening almost every day for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I didn’t mark it on a calendar or anything, but the past few weeks my eating’s been weird.\n\nLike I’ll realize it’s late afternoon and I haven’t had breakfast or lunch, not because I’m trying to skip them, I just… didn’t really get around to it. And then at dinner, even if my wife made something, I’ll kind of pick at it. Which makes me feel bad, honestly, because she’ll make food and I’m sitting there moving it around like a kid.\n\nAnd it’s probably not just in my head, because my jeans have been feeling looser lately. Although one pair always stretched out weird after I washed them wrong, so I don’t know if that counts, but… yeah, the eating being off has been pretty regular for a few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest in doing things you usually enjoy, like playing guitar or spending time with your wife?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, because “loss of interest” sounds like… I don’t care about anything anymore, and I don’t know if that’s exactly it.\n\nLike with guitar, I’ll still think about playing it, but then I’ll get distracted by whether the case latch is sticking or where I left the capo—actually I found it in a jacket pocket once, which made no sense because I don’t even usually put it there. And then the moment kind of passes.\n\nWith my wife, I mean, I still want to be around her. I don’t think I’ve lost interest in her. Sometimes I’m just not as… engaged, maybe? But I don’t know if that’s what you mean or if you mean like I stopped wanting to do those things altogether.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're having more trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It’s not like I’m confused about where I am or anything, but when I try to do something that takes actual focus, I get stuck.\n\nLike if I’m working on some code, I’ll read the same line over and over and still not really know what I’m looking at, even though I know I should. Or with music, if I’m trying to arrange something, I’ll replay the same eight bars a bunch of times and then I still can’t decide what should come next.\n\nAnd then I start thinking maybe the problem is the sound, or the plug-in, or whether I organized the folder wrong—because I have these folders named terribly, honestly—and then I’m not making the decision anymore. I’m just circling around it. So yeah, thinking clearly has felt harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood has been present most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s kind of there when I wake up, like this heavy, flat feeling before anything’s even happened.\n\nThe apartment feels really quiet, even though it’s always kind of quiet in the morning—except the fridge makes this clicking noise sometimes, which I keep meaning to figure out—but lately it just feels… emptier, I guess. And even if I pick up my guitar, which usually changes the mood at least a little, it doesn’t really lift it.\n\nSo yeah, not every single second, but most of the day, most days lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or having trouble starting tasks that used to feel automatic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one, yes. Everything feels kind of slowed down right now.\n\nLike I’ll open my laptop and just sit there with it open for ages before I even put my hands on the keyboard. And then I’ll realize I haven’t plugged in the little MIDI keyboard, or the actual keyboard—well, the computer keyboard is right there, obviously—but the music one, and I’ll just stare at the cable like it’s this whole separate project.\n\nIt’s not that I don’t know how to do it. It just takes me a weirdly long time to start moving, even for things that used to be automatic."
